\displaystyle\sup_{\psi\in\Psi_{J}}\left|{n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\psi(\bm{S}_{i}/A_{n})^{2}-\E[\psi(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n})^{2}]\over\E[\psi(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n})^{2}]}\right|=\left\|\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n-I_{J}\right\|^{2}=o_{p}(1).

(A.9)

This implies that the empirical
L^{2}
norm
\|\cdot\|_{L^{2},n}
and true
L^{2}
norm
\|\cdot\|_{L^{2}(g)}
are equivalent over
\Psi_{J}
with probability approaching one. Then from Corollary A.1 in
Huang, 2003
, we obtain (
A.8
).

Now we show (
A.9
). Recall that
\check{\Psi}_{J}=\E[\psi_{J}(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n})\psi_{J}(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n})^{\prime}]
. Observe that

\displaystyle\sup_{\psi\in\Psi_{J},\E[\psi(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n})^{2}]=1}\left|{1\over n}\!\sum_{i=1}^{n}\!\psi\!\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}}\right)^{2}\!\!-1\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{J},\|\check{\Psi}_{J}^{1/2}\bm{x}\|=1}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!|\bm{x}^{\prime}(\Psi^{\prime}_{J,n}\Psi_{J,n}/n-\check{\Psi}_{J})\bm{x}|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{J},\|\check{\Psi}_{J}^{1/2}\bm{x}\|=1}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!|\bm{x}^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{1/2}(\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1/2}(\Psi^{\prime}_{J,n}\Psi_{J,n}/n)\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1/2}\!-I_{J})\check{\Psi}_{J}^{1/2}\bm{x}|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{J},\|\bm{v}\|=1}|\bm{v}^{\prime}(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n)-I_{J})\bm{v}|

\displaystyle=\|\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n-I_{J}\|^{2}.

Next, we show (
A.8
) when
\Psi_{J}=\text{Wav}(J,R_{0},\varrho)
. This follows from almost the same argument in the proof of Theorem 5.2 in
Chen and Christensen, 2015
.

A.4.
Proof of Proposition
2.2

Observe that

\displaystyle\|\widehat{m}-\widetilde{m}\|_{L^{2}(g)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{J}\left\{(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1})^{-1}-(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n)^{-}\right\}\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}M_{0}/n\|_{L^{2}(g)}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\|\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}_{J}(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1})^{-1}\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}v_{n}/n\|_{L^{2}(g)}^{2}

\displaystyle=2\|\left\{(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1})^{-1}-(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n)^{-}\right\}\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}M_{0}/n\|_{L^{2}(g)}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\|(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1})^{-1}\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}v_{n}/n\|_{L^{2}(g)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1})^{-1}-(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n)^{-}\|^{2}\|\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}M_{0}/n\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\|(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1})^{-1}\|^{2}\|\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}v_{n}/n\|^{2}.

By similar arguments in the proof of Proposition
2.1
, we have

\|(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1})^{-1}-(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n)^{-}\|=O_{p}(\varsigma_{J,n}\lambda_{J}^{-2}),

\|\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}M_{0}/n\|=O_{p}(\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J})
, and
\|\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}v_{n}/n\|=O_{p}(\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}/\sqrt{A_{n}})
. Then we have
\|\widehat{m}-\widetilde{m}\|_{L^{2}(g)}=O_{p}(\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}/\sqrt{A_{n}}+\varsigma_{J,n}\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}^{-1})
. Further, we can show that
\|\widetilde{m}-m_{0}\|_{L^{2}(g)}=O_{p}(\|m_{0}-m_{0,J}\|_{L^{2}(g)})
by the same argument in the proof of Lemma 2.5 in
Chen and Christensen, 2015
.

A.5.
Proof of Corollary
2.2

Let
P_{J}
denote the
L^{2}(g)
orthogonal projection operator onto
\Psi_{J}
given by

P_{J}m(\bm{z})=\psi_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\E\left[\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)^{\prime}\right]^{-1}\E\left[\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)m\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)\right].

Observe that for any
\widetilde{m}\in\Psi_{J}
,

\displaystyle\|m_{0}-m_{J,0}\|_{L^{2}(g)}

\displaystyle=\|m_{0}-\widetilde{m}+\widetilde{m}-m_{J,0}\|_{L^{2}(g)}

\displaystyle\leq\|m_{0}-\widetilde{m}\|_{L^{2}(g)}+\|\widetilde{m}-m_{J,0}\|_{L^{2}(g)}

\displaystyle\leq\|m_{0}-\widetilde{m}\|_{L^{2}(g)}+\|P_{J}(\widetilde{m}-m_{0})\|_{L^{2}(g)}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|m_{0}-\widetilde{m}\|_{L^{2}(g)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|m_{0}-\widetilde{m}\|_{\infty}.

For the second inequality, we used
P_{J}m_{0}=m_{0,J}
. For the third inequality, we used the fact that
P_{J}
is an orthogonal projection on
L^{2}(g)
. For the last wave relation, we used Assumption
2.5
(i). The above inequality holds uniformly in
\widetilde{m}\in\Psi_{J}
. Then by taking the
\inf
over
\widetilde{m}\in\Psi_{J}
, we obtain the desired result.

Supplementary Material

The supplementary material includes the introduction of the spatial regression model with the uniform and
L^{2}
convergence rates, a multivariate CLT, and an estimator of the asymptotic variance for series ridge estimators (Section
B
), proofs for Section
2
(Section
C
), proofs for Section
3
(Section
D
), proofs for Section
B
(Section
E
), and auxiliary lemmas (Section
F
).

Appendix B
Series regression estimators

In this section, we establish the asymptotic properties of series ridge estimators of a spatial regression model. For this, we first introduce our model and series estimators (Sections
B.1
and
B.2
). Then we provide uniform and
L^{2}
convergence rates and a multivariate CLT of general series estimators (Sections
B.2
,
B.4
, and
B.5
), and establish that spline and wavelet estimators attain the optimal uniform and
L^{2}
convergence rates (Sections
B.3
and
B.4
).

B.1.
Spatial regression model

Consider the nonparametric regression model

\displaystyle Y(\bm{S}_{i})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{m}_{0}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{i})\right)+\mathfrak{h}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{i})\right)\varepsilon_{i}

\displaystyle=:\mathfrak{m}_{0}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{i})\right)+\nu_{n,i},\ i=1,\dots,n,

(B.1)

where
\{\bm{S}_{i}\}\subset R_{n}
is a sequence of (stochastic) sampling sites with the density
A_{n}^{-1}g(\cdot/A_{n})
,
\bm{X}:=\{\bm{X}(\bm{s})=(X_{1}(\bm{s}),\dots,X_{p}(\bm{s}))^{\prime}:\bm{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
is a strictly stationary random field defined on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathfrak{m}_{0}:[-1/2,1/2]^{d}\times\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{R}
is the spatial regression function with
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
,
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}
is a sequence of i.i.d. random variables such that
\E[\varepsilon_{i}]=0
and
\E[\varepsilon_{i}^{2}]=1
,
\mathfrak{h}:[-1/2,1/2]^{d}\times\mathcal{X}\to(0,\infty)
is the variance function
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}
. In what follows, we assume that
\bm{X}=\{\bm{X}(\bm{s}):\bm{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
,
\{\bm{S}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, and
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are mutually independent. The model (
B.1
) can be seen as a nonlinear extension of geographically weighted regression models (cf.
Lu et al., 2014
and
Gollini et al., 2015
). In our spatial regression model, we assume that the spatial correlation of the response variable
Y(\bm{S}_{i})
is explained by the covariate process
\bm{X}
. Therefore,
\nu_{n,i}
can be interpreted as an exogenous variable that represents the observation error at each location or does not affect the conditional expectation of the response variable given the covariate vector
\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{i})
.

For the model (
B.1
), we assume the following conditions.

Assumption B.1
.

For
j=1,\dots,d
, let
\{A_{n1,j}\}_{n\geq 1}
and
\{A_{n2,j}\}_{n\geq 1}
be sequences of positive numbers such that
\min\{A_{n2,j},{A_{n1,j}\over A_{n2,j}}\}\to\infty
and
A_{n}(A_{n}^{(1)}\log n)^{-1}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
where
A_{n}^{(1)}=\prod_{j=1}^{d}A_{n1,j}
.

(i)

The random field
\bm{X}=\{\bm{X}(\bm{s})=(X_{1}(\bm{s}),\dots,X_{p}(\bm{s}))^{\prime}:\bm{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
is strictly stationary and
\mathcal{X}
is convex and has nonempty interior.

(ii)

The random field
\bm{X}
is
\beta
-mixing with mixing coefficients
\beta(a;b)
such that as
n\to\infty
,
A_{n}(A_{n}^{(1)})^{-1}\beta(\underline{A}_{n2};A_{n})\to 0
where
\underline{A}_{n2}=\min_{1\leq j\leq d}A_{n2,j}
.

(iii)

\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
is a sequence of i.i.d. random variables such that
\E[\varepsilon_{1}]=0
,
\E[\varepsilon_{1}^{2}]=1
, and
\E[|\varepsilon_{1}|^{q}]<\infty
for some
q>2
.

(iv)

The function
\mathfrak{h}
is continuous on
R_{0}\times\mathcal{X}
and
\sup_{(\bm{z},\bm{x})\in R_{0}\times\mathcal{X}}|\mathfrak{h}(\bm{z},\bm{x})|<\infty
.

By a similar argument in the proof of Proposition
3.1
, we can confirm that Assumption
B.1
is satisfied for a certain class of Lévy-driven moving average random fields.

To allow possibly unbounded support
\mathcal{X}
, we construct a series estimator of
\mathfrak{m}_{0}
over
R_{0}\times D_{n}
that satisfies the following conditions.

Assumption B.2
.

Let
D_{n}
be compact, convex, and have a nonempty interior.

(i)

D_{n}\subset D_{n+1}\subset\mathcal{X}
for all
n
.

(ii)

There exist
v_{1},v_{2}>0
such that
N(D_{n},\|\cdot\|,\delta)\lesssim n^{v_{1}}\delta^{-v_{2}}
.

If
\mathcal{X}=\mathbb{R}^{p}
, one can take
D_{n}=\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:\|\bm{x}\|\leq r_{n}\}
or
[-r_{n},r_{n}]^{p}
where
\{r_{n}\}
is a sequence of constants such that
r_{n}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
. We can cover these
D_{n}
with
(2r_{n}/\delta)^{p}

\ell^{\infty}
-balls of radius
\delta
, each of which is contained in an Euclidean ball of radius
\delta\sqrt{p}
. Then we have
N(D_{n},\|\cdot\|,\delta)\leq(2\sqrt{p}r_{n}/\delta)^{p}\lesssim n^{vp}\delta^{-p}
for some
v>0
.

B.2.
Uniform convergence rates

We estimate the regression function
\mathfrak{m}_{0}
by the following series ridge estimator.

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}(\bm{z},\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}_{n}(\bm{z},\bm{x}):=b_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z},\bm{x})^{\prime}\left({B_{J,n}^{(w)^{\prime}}B_{J,n}^{(w)}\over n}+\varsigma_{J,n}I_{J}\right)^{-1}{B_{J,n}^{(w)^{\prime}}\bm{Y}\over n},

\displaystyle(\bm{z}^{\prime},\bm{x}^{\prime})^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(z_{1},\dots,z_{d},x_{1},\dots,x_{p})^{\prime}\in R_{0}\times D_{n},

where
\varsigma_{J,n}
is a sequence of positive constants with
\varsigma_{J,n}\to 0
as
n,J\to\infty
,
\bm{Y}=(Y(\bm{S}_{1}),\dots,Y(\bm{S}_{n}))^{\prime}
,
b_{J,1},\dots,b_{J,J}
are a collection of
J
sieve basis functions,

\displaystyle b_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z},\bm{x})

\displaystyle=(b_{J,1}(\bm{z},\bm{x})w_{n}(\bm{x}),\dots,b_{J,J}(\bm{z},\bm{x})w_{n}(\bm{x}))^{\prime},\ w_{n}(\bm{x})=\begin{cases}1&\text{if $\bm{x}\in D_{n}$},\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

\displaystyle B_{J,n}^{(w)}

\displaystyle=\left(b_{J}^{(w)}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{1})\right),\dots,b_{J}^{(w)}\left({\bm{S}_{n}\over A_{n}},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{n})\right)\right)^{\prime}.

In our study, we use the above weight function
w_{n}(\bm{x})
, but other candidates for the weight function include
w_{n}(\bm{x})=(1+\|\bm{x}\|^{2})^{-w}
and
w_{n}(\bm{x})=\exp(-\|\bm{x}\|^{w})
with some
w\geq 0
. In fact, in
Lee and Robinson, 2016
consider a (non-penalized) series estimator with
w_{n}(\bm{x})=(1+\|\bm{x}\|^{2})^{-w}
. We leave the extension of our theoretical results when using other weight functions for future research.

In Section
B.3
, we focus on the case where the basis functions are supported on
D_{n}=D_{0}=[-1/2,1/2]^{p}
. In this case, we can construct tensor product B-spline or wavelet bases supported on
R_{0}\times D_{0}
in the same manner as in Section
2.5
. For example, the spline basis functions
\psi_{J_{0,k},j}(v)
on
v\in[-1/2,1/2]
,
j=1,\dots,J_{0,k}
,
k=1,\dots,d+p
are extended to the basis functions on
(\bm{z},\bm{x})=(z_{1},\dots,z_{d},x_{1},\dots,x_{p})^{\prime}\in[-1/2,1/2]^{d+p}
by
\psi_{J_{0,1},j_{1}}(z_{1})\times\dots\times\psi_{J_{0,d},j_{d}}(z_{d})\times\psi_{J_{0,d+1},j_{d+1}}(x_{1})\times\dots\times\psi_{J_{0,d+p},j_{d+p}}(x_{p})
,

j_{k}=1,\dots,J_{0,k}
,
k=1,\dots,d+p
. In the case where
D_{n}=[-r_{n},r_{n}]^{p}
, we can construct basis functions supported on
R_{0}\times D_{n}
by rescaling the components corresponding to
D_{0}
in basis functions supported on
R_{0}\times D_{0}
.

Define

\zeta_{J,n}=\sup_{(\bm{z},\bm{x})\in R_{0}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}}\|b_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z},\bm{x})\|,\ \lambda_{J,n}=\lambda_{\text{min}}\left(\E\left[b_{J}^{(w)}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{1})\right)b_{J}^{(w)}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{1})\right)^{\prime}\right]\right)^{-1/2}.

We assume the following conditions on the sieve basis functions.

Assumption B.3
.

Let
\nabla b_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z},\bm{x})=\left({\partial b_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z},\bm{x})\over\partial\bm{z}},{\partial b_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z},\bm{x})\over\partial\bm{x}}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{J\times(d+p)}
where

\displaystyle{\partial b_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z},\bm{x})\over\partial\bm{z}}

\displaystyle\!=\!\left(\!{\partial b_{J,j}(\bm{z},\bm{x})w_{n}(\bm{x})\over\partial z_{k}}\!\right)_{1\leq j\leq J,1\leq k\leq d},{\partial b_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z},\bm{x})\over\partial\bm{x}}\!=\!\left(\!{\partial b_{J,j}(\bm{z},\bm{x})w_{n}(\bm{x})\over\partial x_{k}}\!\right)_{1\leq j\leq J,1\leq k\leq p}.

(i)

There exist
\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\geq 0
such that
\sup_{(\bm{z},\bm{x})\in R_{0}\times D_{n}}\|\nabla b_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z},\bm{x})\|\lesssim n^{\omega_{1}}J^{\omega_{2}}
.

(ii)

There exist
\varpi_{1}\geq 0
,
\varpi_{2}>0
such that
\zeta_{J,n}\lesssim n^{\varpi_{1}}J^{\varpi_{2}}
.

(iii)

\lambda_{\rm{min}}(\E[b_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{1}))b_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{1}))^{\prime}])>0
for each
J
and
n
.

Assumption
B.3
is satisfied with
\lambda_{J,n}\!\lesssim\!1
and
\zeta_{J,n}\!\lesssim\!\sqrt{J}
for tensor-products of univariate polynomial spline, trigonometric polynomial or wavelet bases. See also the comments on Assumption
2.3
.

Let
\widetilde{b}_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z},\bm{x})
denote the orthonormalized vector of basis functions, that is,

\widetilde{b}_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z},\bm{x})=\E\left[b_{J}^{(w)}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{1})\right)b_{J}^{(w)}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{1})\right)^{\prime}\right]^{-1/2}b_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z},\bm{x}),

and
\widetilde{B}_{J,n}^{(w)}=(\widetilde{b}_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{1})),\dots,\widetilde{b}_{J}(\bm{S}_{n}/A_{n},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{n})))^{\prime}
.

Define
B_{J}^{(w)}
as the closed linear span of
\{b_{J,1}w_{n},\dots,b_{J,J}w_{n}\}
in
L^{2}(R_{0}\times D_{n})
. Let
P_{J,n}^{(w)}
be the empirical projection operator onto
B_{J}^{(w)}
, that is,

\displaystyle P_{J,n}^{(w)}\mathfrak{m}(\bm{z},\bm{x})

\displaystyle=b_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z},\bm{x})^{\prime}\left({B_{J,n}^{(w)^{\prime}}B_{J,n}^{(w)}\over n}\right)^{-}{1\over n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{J}^{(w)}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{i})\right)\mathfrak{m}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{i})\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{b}_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z},\bm{x})(\widetilde{B}_{J,n}^{(w)^{\prime}}\widetilde{B}_{J,n}^{(w)})^{-}\widetilde{B}_{J,n}^{(w)^{\prime}}\mathfrak{M}

where
\mathfrak{M}=(\mathfrak{m}(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{1})),\dots,\mathfrak{m}(\bm{S}_{n}/A_{n},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{n})))^{\prime}
. The operator
P_{J,n}^{(w)}
is well defined:

if
L_{w,n}^{2}(R_{0}\times\mathbb{R}^{p})
denotes the space of functions with norm
\|\cdot\|_{L^{2},w,n}
where

\|f\|_{L^{2},w,n}^{2}={1\over n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(\bm{S}_{i}/A_{n},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{i}))^{2}w_{n}(\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{i})),

then
P_{J,n}^{(w)}:L_{w,n}^{2}(R_{0}\times\mathbb{R}^{p})\to L_{w,n}^{2}(R_{0}\times\mathbb{R}^{p})
is an orthogonal projection onto
B_{J}^{(w)}
whenever
B_{J,n}^{(w)^{\prime}}B_{J,n}^{(w)}
is invertible.

Let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}
denote the projection of
\mathfrak{m}_{0}
onto
B_{J}^{(w)}
under the empirical measure, that is,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}(\bm{z},\bm{x})

\displaystyle=P_{J,n}^{(w)}\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\bm{z},\bm{x})=\widetilde{b}_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z},\bm{x})^{\prime}(\widetilde{B}_{J,n}^{(w)^{\prime}}\widetilde{B}_{J,n}^{(w)})^{-}\widetilde{B}_{J,n}^{(w)^{\prime}}\mathfrak{M}_{0}

where
\mathfrak{M}_{0}=\mathfrak{M}_{0,n}=(\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{1})),\dots,\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\bm{S}_{n}/A_{n},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{n}))^{\prime}
.

The following result provides uniform convergence rates of the variance term of general series estimators.

Proposition B.1
.

Suppose that Assumptions
2.1
(i), (ii),
B.1
,
B.2
, and
B.3
hold. Assume that
\bm{X}=\{\bm{X}(\bm{s}):\bm{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
,
\{\bm{S}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, and
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are mutually independent,
\max\{(\zeta_{J,n}\lambda_{J,n})^{{q\over q-2}},\zeta_{J,n}^{2}(\zeta_{J,n}^{2}+\zeta_{J,n}\lambda_{J,n}^{-1}+\lambda_{J,n}^{2})\}\lesssim\sqrt{A_{n}/(\log n)^{2}}
, and
\varsigma_{J,n}\lesssim\zeta_{J,n}^{2}\sqrt{\log n/A_{n}}
as
n,J\to\infty
. Then

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}-\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\zeta_{J,n}\lambda_{J,n}\sqrt{{\log n\over n}}+\varsigma_{J,n}\zeta_{J,n}^{2}\|\mathfrak{m}_{0}\|_{w,\infty}\right)\ \text{as $n,J\to\infty$},

(B.2)

where
\|\cdot\|_{w,\infty}
denote the weighted sup norm, that is,
\|f\|_{w,\infty}=\sup_{(\bm{z},\bm{x})\in R_{0}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}}|f(\bm{z},\bm{x})w_{n}(\bm{x})|=\sup_{(\bm{z},\bm{x})\in R_{0}\times D_{n}}|f(\bm{z},\bm{x})|
.

Note that
\|\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}-\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}\|_{\infty}=\|\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}-\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}\|_{w,\infty}
since both
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}
have support
R_{0}\times D_{n}
. The convergence rate of the first term in (
B.2
) is the same as the case of i.i.d. data and this is attributed to the fact that the error terms are conditionally independent in the model (
B.1
).

Now we give uniform convergence rates of
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}
. Let
L_{w,n}^{\infty}(R_{0}\times\mathbb{R}^{p})
denote the space of functions of which
\|f\|_{w,\infty}<\infty
and let

\displaystyle\|P_{J,n}^{(w)}\|_{w,\infty}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathfrak{m}\in L_{w,n}^{\infty}(R_{0}\times\mathbb{R}^{p}),\|\mathfrak{m}\|_{w,\infty}\neq 0}{\|P_{J,n}^{(w)}\mathfrak{m}\|_{w,\infty}\over\|\mathfrak{m}\|_{w,\infty}}

denote the (weighted sup) operator norm of
P_{J,n}^{(w)}
.

The following result establishes uniform convergence rates of general series estimators.

Corollary B.1
.

Suppose that assumptions in Proposition
B.1
hold. Then: (1)

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}-\mathfrak{m}_{0}\|_{w,\infty}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\!\left(\!\zeta_{J,n}\lambda_{J,n}\sqrt{{\log n\over n}}+\varsigma_{J,n}\zeta_{J,n}^{2}\|\mathfrak{m}_{0}\|_{w,\infty}\!\right)\!+\!(1\!+\!\|P_{J,n}^{(w)}\|_{w,\infty})\inf_{\mathfrak{m}\in B_{J}^{(w)}}\!\|\mathfrak{m}_{0}-\mathfrak{m}\|_{w,\infty}.

(2) Further, if the linear sieve satisfies
\zeta_{J,n}\lesssim\sqrt{J}
,
\lambda_{J,n}\lesssim 1
, and
\|P_{J,n}^{(w)}\|_{w,\infty}=O_{p}(1)
, then

\|\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}-\mathfrak{m}_{0}\|_{w,\infty}=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{{J\log n\over n}}+\varsigma_{J,n}J\|\mathfrak{m}_{0}\|_{w,\infty}+\inf_{\mathfrak{m}\in B_{J}^{(w)}}\|\mathfrak{m}_{0}-\mathfrak{m}\|_{w,\infty}\right).

Note that the term
(1\!+\!\|P_{J,n}^{(w)}\|_{w,\infty})\inf_{\mathfrak{m}\in B_{J}^{(w)}}\!\|\mathfrak{m}_{0}-\mathfrak{m}\|_{w,\infty}
comes from the bound of
\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}-\mathfrak{m}_{0}\|_{\infty}
, which can be addressed in a similar way to the proof of Lemma 2.4 in
Chen and Christensen, 2015
. The main difference from Lemma 2.4 in
Chen and Christensen, 2015
arises from the stochastic term associated with the evaluation of
\|\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}-\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}_{0}\|_{\infty}
. In particular, the term
\varsigma_{J,n}\zeta_{J,n}^{2}\|\mathfrak{m}_{0}\|_{w,\infty}
, which is due to the ridge penalty, has emerged as an additional term. The evaluation of this term requires a new inequality concerning the spectral norm of the difference between the inverses of two non-singular matrices (Lemma
F.5
).

B.3.
Optimal uniform rates for spline and wavelet estimators

In this subsection, we establish that the spline and wavelet estimators attain the optimal uniform convergence rate when the regression function belongs to a Hölder space.

Assumption B.4
.

Assume that
\bm{X}(\bm{0})
has the density function
f_{\bm{X}}
.

(i)

D_{n}=D_{0}=[-1/2,1/2]^{p}\subset\mathcal{X}
for all
n
.

(ii)

The density functions
g
and
f_{\bm{X}}
are uniformly bounded away from zero and infinity on
R_{0}
and
D_{0}
, respectively.

(iii)

The restriction of
\mathfrak{m}_{0}
to
R_{0}\times D_{0}
belongs to
\Lambda^{r}(R_{0}\times D_{0})
for some
r>0
.

(iv)

Let
B_{J}
be the closed linear span of
\{b_{J,1},\dots,b_{J,J}\}
. The sieve
B_{J}
is
\text{BSpl}(J,R_{0}\times D_{0},\varrho)
or
\text{Wav}(J,R_{0}\times D_{0},\varrho)
with
\varrho>\max\{r,1\}
.

One can verify that Condition (i) implies Assumption
B.2
and Conditions (ii) and (iv) imply Assumption
B.3
with
\zeta_{J,n}\lesssim\sqrt{J}
and
\lambda_{J,n}\sim 1
. Note that given the spline or wavelet basis functions on
[-1/2,1/2]
, we can construct basis functions on
R_{0}
by taking the product of the elements of each of the univariate bases. See also the discussion before Assumption
2.5
for the construction of multivariate basis functions.

The following result establishes that the spline and wavelet estimators achieve the optimal uniform convergence rates of
Stone, 1982
.

Theorem B.1
.

Suppose that Assumptions
2.1
(i), (ii),
B.1
,
B.2
, and
B.4
hold. Assume that
\bm{X}=\{\bm{X}(\bm{s}):\bm{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
,
\{\bm{S}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, and
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are mutually independent,
2r\geq 3d+p
,
\max\{J^{{q\over 2(q-2)}},J^{2}\}\lesssim\sqrt{A_{n}/(\log n)^{2}}
, and
\varsigma_{J,n}(\|\mathfrak{m}_{0}\|_{w,\infty}\sqrt{J^{2}n/(\log n)}+\sqrt{A_{n}/(J^{2}\log n)})\lesssim 1
as
n,J\to\infty
.

If
J\sim\left(n/\log n\right)^{{d+p\over 2r+d+p}}
, then

\|\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}-\mathfrak{m}_{0}\|_{w,\infty}=O_{p}\left(\left({\log n\over n}\right)^{r\over 2r+d+p}\right).

B.4.
L^{2}
convergence rates

Let
L^{p}(\bm{S},\bm{X})
denote the function space consisting of all (equivalence class) of measurable functions
f
for which the
L^{p}(\bm{S},\bm{X})
norm
\|f\|_{L^{p}(\bm{S},\bm{X})}\!=\!\E\left[|f(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{1}))|^{p}\right]^{1/p}
is finite.

The next result provides a sharp upper bound of the
L^{2}
convergence rates of general series estimators for the spatial regression model. Note that mixing conditions for the random field
\bm{X}
are not necessary to establish the result.

Proposition B.2
.

Suppose that Assumptions
2.1
(i), (ii),
B.1
, and
B.3
(iii) hold. Additionally, assume that
\bm{X}=\{\bm{X}(\bm{s}):\bm{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
,
\{\bm{S}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, and
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are mutually independent,
\varsigma_{J,n}\lesssim\zeta_{J,n}^{2}\sqrt{\log n/n}
, and
\zeta_{J,n}^{3}(\zeta_{J,n}+\lambda_{J,n}^{-1})\lesssim\sqrt{A_{n}/(\log n)^{2}}
. Then

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}-\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}\|_{L^{2}(\bm{S},\bm{X})}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left({\zeta_{J,n}\lambda_{J,n}\over\sqrt{n}}+\varsigma_{J,n}\zeta_{J,n}\lambda_{J,n}^{-1}\|\mathfrak{m}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\bm{S},\bm{X})}\right),

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}-\mathfrak{m}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\bm{S},\bm{X})}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\|\mathfrak{m}_{0}-\mathfrak{m}_{0,J}\|_{L^{2}(\bm{S},\bm{X})}\right),

where
\mathfrak{m}_{0,J}
is the
L^{2}(\bm{S},\bm{X})
orthogonal projection of
\mathfrak{m}_{0}
onto
B_{J}^{(w)}
.

The following result establishes that the spline and wavelet estimators attain the optimal
L^{2}
convergence rates of
Stone, 1982
.

Corollary B.2
.

Suppose that Assumptions
2.1
(i), (ii),
B.1
, and
B.4
hold. Additionally, assume that
\bm{X}=\{\bm{X}(\bm{s}):\bm{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
,
\{\bm{S}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, and
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are mutually independent,
2r\geq 3d+p
,
\|\mathfrak{m}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\bm{S},\bm{X})}<\infty
,
\varsigma_{J,n}(\sqrt{n}\lambda_{J,n}^{-2}+\sqrt{n/(J^{2}\log n)})\lesssim 1
, and
J^{2}\lesssim\sqrt{A_{n}/(\log n)^{2}}
. If
J\sim n^{{d+p\over 2r+d+p}}
, then

\|\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}-\mathfrak{m}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\bm{S},\bm{X})}=O_{p}\left(n^{-{r\over 2r+d+p}}\right).

B.5.
Asymptotic normality

The following result establishes the asymptotic normality of the series estimator with an arbitrary basis.

Theorem B.2
.

Let
\{(\bm{z}_{\ell},\bm{x}_{\ell})\}_{1\leq\ell\leq L}
be a set of points such that
\bm{z}_{\ell}\in R_{0}
and
\bm{x}_{\ell}
are any interior points of
\mathcal{X}
.
Suppose that Assumptions
2.1
(i), (ii),
B.1
, and
B.3
(iii) hold. Additionally, assume that
\bm{X}=\{\bm{X}(\bm{s}):\bm{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
,
\{\bm{S}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, and
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are mutually independent,
\inf_{(\bm{z},\bm{x})\in R_{0}\times\mathcal{X}}\mathfrak{h}(\bm{z},\bm{x})>0
, and

(a)

\max\{(\zeta_{J,n}\lambda_{J,n})^{{2q\over q-2}},(\zeta_{J,n}\lambda_{J,n})^{4},\zeta_{J,n}^{3}\lambda_{J,n}^{-1}\}\lesssim\sqrt{A_{n}/(\log n)^{2}}
,

(b)

\|P_{J,n}^{(w)}\|_{w,\infty}=O_{p}(1)
,
\sqrt{n}(\min_{1\leq\ell\leq L}\|\widetilde{b}_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z}_{\ell},\bm{x}_{\ell})\|)^{-1}\inf_{\mathfrak{m}\in B_{J}^{(w)}}\|\mathfrak{m}_{0}-\mathfrak{m}\|_{\infty}\to 0
,

(c)

\varsigma_{J,n}\lesssim\zeta_{J,n}^{2}\sqrt{\log n/A_{n}}
,
\varsigma_{J,n}\zeta_{J,n}^{2}\sqrt{n}\|\mathfrak{m}_{0}\|_{w,\infty}(\min_{1\leq\ell\leq L}\|\widetilde{b}_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z}_{\ell},\bm{x}_{\ell})\|)^{-1}\to 0

as
n,J\to\infty
. Then we have

\sqrt{n}V_{J}^{-{1\over 2}}\left({\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}(\bm{z}_{1},\bm{x}_{1})-\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\bm{z}_{1},\bm{x}_{1})\over\sqrt{V_{J}(\bm{z}_{1},\bm{x}_{1})}},\dots,{\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}(\bm{z}_{L},\bm{x}_{L})-\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\bm{z}_{L},\bm{x}_{L})\over\sqrt{V_{J}(\bm{z}_{L},\bm{x}_{L})}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}N(\bm{0},I_{L})\ \text{as $n,J\to\infty$},

where

\displaystyle V_{J}

\displaystyle=(V_{J}^{(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})})_{1\leq\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\leq L},\ V_{J}^{(\ell_{1},\ell_{2})}={V_{J}(\bm{z}_{\ell_{1}},\bm{x}_{\ell_{1}},\bm{z}_{\ell_{2}},\bm{x}_{\ell_{2}})\over\sqrt{V_{J}(\bm{z}_{\ell_{1}},\bm{x}_{\ell_{1}})}\sqrt{V_{J}(\bm{z}_{\ell_{2}},\bm{x}_{\ell_{2}})}},

\displaystyle V_{J}(\bm{z},\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{b}_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z},\bm{x})^{\prime}H_{J}\widetilde{b}_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z},\bm{x}),\ V_{J}(\bm{z}_{\ell_{1}},\bm{x}_{\ell_{1}},\bm{z}_{\ell_{2}},\bm{x}_{\ell_{2}})=\widetilde{b}_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z}_{\ell_{1}},\bm{x}_{\ell_{1}})^{\prime}H_{J}\widetilde{b}_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z}_{\ell_{2}},\bm{x}_{\ell_{2}}),

\displaystyle H_{J}

\displaystyle=\E\left[\mathfrak{h}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{1})\right)^{2}\widetilde{b}_{J}^{(w)}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{1})\right)\widetilde{b}_{J}^{(w)}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{1})\right)^{\prime}\right].

Conditions (a) and (c) are concerned with replacing
(\widetilde{B}^{(w)^{\prime}}_{J,n}\widetilde{B}^{(w)}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}(\check{B}_{J}^{(w)})^{-1})^{-1}
in the definition of
\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}
with
I_{J}
where
\check{B}_{J}^{(w)}=\E[b_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{1}))b_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{1}))^{\prime}]
. Condition (b) is required to show that the bias term of the series estimator is asymptotically negligible.

Now we provide a consistent estimator of
V_{J}(\bm{z}_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\bm{z}_{2},\bm{x}_{2})
for
(\bm{z}_{1},\bm{x}_{1}),(\bm{z}_{2},\bm{x}_{2})\in R_{0}\times D_{0}
, which enables us to construct confidence intervals for
\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\bm{z},\bm{x})
. As with the spatial trend regression model, it should be noted that in the spatial regression model as well, the series estimator is not affected by boundary estimation of
R_{0}
on the support of the regression function, unlike the kernel estimator. For a more detailed discussion, refer to the discussion following Theorem
2.2
.

Define
\widehat{V}_{J}(\bm{z}_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\bm{z}_{2},\bm{x}_{2}):=b_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z}_{1},\bm{x}_{1})^{\prime}\widehat{H}_{J}b_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z}_{2},\bm{x}_{2})
where

\displaystyle\widehat{H}_{J}

\displaystyle={1\over n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left({B_{J,n}^{(w)^{\prime}}B_{J,n}^{(w)}\over n}+\varsigma_{J,n}I_{J}\right)^{-1}\!\!\!\!\!b_{J}^{(w)}\!\!\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{i})\right)\!b_{J}^{(w)}\!\!\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{i})\right)^{\prime}\!\!\left({B_{J,n}^{(w)^{\prime}}B_{J,n}^{(w)}\over n}+\varsigma_{J,n}I_{J}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\left(Y(\bm{S}_{i})-\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}},\bm{X}(\bm{S}_{i})\right)\!\right)^{2}.

Proposition B.3
.

Let
2r\geq 3d+p
. Assume that
\bm{X}=\{\bm{X}(\bm{s}):\bm{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
,
\{\bm{S}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
, and
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are mutually independent and
\varsigma_{J,n}(\|\mathfrak{m}_{0}\|_{w,\infty}\sqrt{J^{2}n/(\log n)}+\sqrt{A_{n}/(J^{2}\log n)})\lesssim 1
as
n,J\to\infty
. Suppose that
Assumptions
2.1
(i), (ii),
B.1
,
B.3
(iii), and
B.4
hold. Additionally, suppose that Conditions (a)-(c) in Theorem
B.2
hold and
\inf_{(\bm{z},\bm{x})\in R_{0}\times D_{0}}\mathfrak{h}(\bm{z},\bm{x})>0
. Then, for
(\bm{z}_{j},\bm{x}_{j}),j=1,2\in R_{0}\times D_{0}
,
\|\widetilde{b}_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z}_{1},\bm{x}_{1})\|^{-1}(\widehat{V}_{J}(\bm{z}_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\bm{z}_{2},\bm{x}_{2})-V_{J}(\bm{z}_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\bm{z}_{2},\bm{x}_{2}))\|\widetilde{b}_{J}^{(w)}(\bm{z}_{2},\bm{x}_{2})\|^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}0
as
n,J\to\infty
.

Theorem
B.2
and Proposition
B.3
enable us to construct confidence intervals of
\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\bm{z},\bm{x})
. Define
\widehat{V}_{J}(\bm{z},\bm{x}):=\widehat{V}_{J}(\bm{z},\bm{x},\bm{z},\bm{x})
and consider a confidence interval of the form

\displaystyle\widehat{C}_{1-\tau}(\bm{z},\bm{x})=\left[\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}(\bm{z},\bm{x})-\sqrt{{\widehat{V}_{J}(\bm{z},\bm{x})\over n}}q_{1-\tau/2},\widehat{\mathfrak{m}}(\bm{z},\bm{x})+\sqrt{{\widehat{V}_{J}(\bm{z},\bm{x})\over n}}q_{1-\tau/2}\right]

for
\tau\in(0,1)
,
(\bm{z},\bm{x})\in R_{0}\times D_{0}
where
q_{1-\tau}
is the
(1-\tau)
-quantile of the standard normal random variable. Then under assumptions in Proposition
B.3
, we have
\Prob(\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\bm{z},\bm{x})\in\widehat{C}_{1-\tau}(\bm{z},\bm{x}))\to 1-\tau
as
n,J\to\infty
.

Appendix C
Proofs for Section
2

C.1.
The remaining proof of Proposition
2.1

Now we provide proofs for Steps 3 and 4 of Proposition
2.1
.

(Step 3) Now we show that
P_{n,2}=o(1)
as
n\to\infty
. Define
e_{n}=(e_{n,1},\dots,e_{n,n})^{\prime}
and
\varepsilon_{n}=(\varepsilon_{n,1},\dots,\varepsilon_{n,n})^{\prime}
. Observe that

\displaystyle P_{n,2}

\displaystyle\leq\!\Prob\left(\!\max_{\bm{z}_{n}\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\!\!\left|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}_{n})^{\prime}\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}e_{n}/n\right|\!\geq\!C\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\!\sqrt{{\log n\over A_{n}}}\right)\!+\Prob\left(\!\max_{\bm{z}_{n}\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\!\!\left|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}_{n})^{\prime}\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\varepsilon_{n}/n\right|\!\geq\!C\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\!\sqrt{{\log n\over A_{n}}}\right)

\displaystyle=:P_{n,21}+P_{n,22}.

Now we show that
P_{n,21}=o(1)
as
n,J\to\infty
. We can show that
P_{n,22}=o(1)
from almost the same argument in the proof of Lemma 3.2 in
Chen and Christensen, 2015
. Let
\tau_{n}=n^{1/q}(\log n)^{\iota}
for some
\iota>0
. Define
e_{n,i}^{(1)}=\eta(\bm{S}_{i}/A_{n})e(\bm{s}_{i})1\{|e(\bm{S}_{i})|\leq\tau_{n}\}
,
e_{n,i}^{(2)}=\eta(\bm{S}_{i}/A_{n})e(\bm{s}_{i})-e_{n,i}^{(1)}
,
e_{n}^{(1)}=(e_{n,1}^{(1)},\dots,e_{n,n}^{(1)})^{\prime}
,
e_{n}^{(2)}=(e_{n,1}^{(2)},\dots,e_{n,n}^{(2)})^{\prime}
.
Further, define
\Phi_{n,j}(\bm{z})=\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}e_{n}^{(j)}/n-\E[\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}e_{n}^{(j)}/n]
,
j=1,2
. Then we have

\displaystyle P_{n,21}

\displaystyle\leq\Prob\left(\max_{\bm{z}_{n}\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\left|\Phi_{n,1}(\bm{z}_{n})\right|\geq{C\over 2}\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\sqrt{{\log n\over A_{n}}}\right)+\Prob\left(\max_{\bm{z}_{n}\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\left|\Phi_{n,2}(\bm{z}_{n})\right|\geq{C\over 2}\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\sqrt{{\log n\over A_{n}}}\right)

\displaystyle=:P_{n,211}+P_{n,212}.

First, we control
P_{n,212}
. Note that

\displaystyle P_{n,212}

\displaystyle\leq\Prob\left(\max_{\bm{z}_{n}\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\left|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}_{n})^{\prime}\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}e_{n}^{(2)}/n\right|\geq{C\over 4}\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\sqrt{{\log n\over A_{n}}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\Prob\left(\max_{\bm{z}_{n}\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\left|\E\left[\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}_{n})^{\prime}\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}e_{n}^{(2)}/n\right]\right|\geq{C\over 4}\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\sqrt{{\log n\over A_{n}}}\right).

Observe that

\displaystyle\Prob\left(\max_{\bm{z}_{n}\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\left|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}_{n})^{\prime}\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}e_{n}^{(2)}/n\right|\geq{C\over 4}\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\sqrt{{\log n\over A_{n}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Prob\left(|e(\bm{S}_{i})|>\tau_{n}\ \text{for some $i=1,\dots,n$}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\tau_{n}^{-q}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\E[|e(\bm{S}_{i})|^{q}]=n\tau_{n}^{-q}=(\log n)^{-q\iota}\to 0.

(C.1)

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\E\left[\left|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}e_{n}^{(2)}/n\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq{1\over n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\E\left[\left|\psi_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}}\right)e_{n,i}^{(2)}\right|\right]\lesssim\E\left[\left|\psi_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}}\right)\right||e(\bm{S}_{i})|1\{|e(\bm{S}_{i})|>\tau_{n}\}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim{\zeta_{J}^{2}\lambda_{J}^{2}\over\tau_{n}^{q-1}}\E\left[\E\left[|e(\bm{S}_{1})|^{q}|\bm{S}_{1}\right]\right]\lesssim{\zeta_{J}^{2}\lambda_{J}^{2}\over\tau_{n}^{q-1}}\lesssim\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\sqrt{{\log n\over A_{n}}}

(C.2)

uniformly over
\bm{z}\in R_{0}
. For the last wave relation, we used Assumption
2.4
(ii). Then (
C.1
) and (
C.2
) yield that
P_{n,212}
can be made arbitrarily small for large enough
C\geq 1
.

Next, we control
P_{n,211}
. For this, we introduce some notations. Define
\Gamma_{n}(\bm{\ell};\bm{0})=\prod_{j=1}^{d}((\ell_{j}-1/2)A_{n3,j},(\ell_{j}+1/2)A_{n3,j}]
with
A_{n3,j}=A_{n1,j}+A_{n2,j}
, and the following hypercubes,
\Gamma_{n}(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})=\prod_{j=1}^{d}I_{j}(\Delta_{j})
,
\bm{\Delta}=(\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{d})^{\prime}\in\{1,2\}^{d}
, where

\displaystyle I_{j}(\Delta_{j})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}((\ell_{j}-1/2)A_{n3,j},(\ell_{j}-1/2)A_{n3,j}+A_{n1,j}]&\text{if $\Delta_{j}=1$},\\
((\ell_{j}-1/2)A_{n3,j}+A_{n1,j},(\ell_{j}+1/2)A_{n3,j}]&\text{if $\Delta_{j}=2$}.\end{cases}

Let
\bm{\Delta}_{0}=(1,\dots,1)^{\prime}
. The partitions
\Gamma_{n}(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta}_{0})
correspond to “large blocks” and the partitions
\Gamma_{n}(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})
for
\bm{\Delta}\neq\bm{\Delta}_{0}
correspond to “small blocks”.
Let
L_{n1}=\{\bm{\ell}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:\Gamma_{n}(\bm{\ell};\bm{0})\subset R_{n}\}
denote the index set of all hypercubes
\Gamma_{n}(\bm{\ell};\bm{0})
that are contained in
R_{n}
, and let
L_{n2}=\{\bm{\ell}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:\Gamma_{n}(\bm{\ell};\bm{0})\cap R_{n}\neq\emptyset,\Gamma_{n}(\bm{\ell};\bm{0})\cap R_{n}^{c}\neq\emptyset\}
be the index set of boundary hypercubes.

Define
\phi_{n,i}(\bm{z})=\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\widetilde{\psi}_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}}\right)e_{n,i}^{(1)}-\E\left[\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\widetilde{\psi}_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}}\right)e_{n,i}^{(1)}\right]
.
Note that

\displaystyle n\Phi_{n,1}(\bm{z})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{n,i}(\bm{z})=\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\!\!\!\!\phi_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta}_{0})}(\bm{z})\!+\!\sum_{\bm{\Delta}\neq\bm{\Delta}_{0}}\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\!\!\!\!\phi_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})\!+\!\sum_{\bm{\Delta}\in\{1,2\}^{d}}\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n2}}\!\!\!\!\phi_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z}),

where
\phi_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{n,i}(\bm{z})1\{\bm{S}_{i}\in\Gamma_{n}(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})\cap R_{n}\}
.

For
\bm{\Delta}\in\{1,2\}^{d}
, let
\{\widetilde{\phi}_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})\}_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}\cup L_{n2}}
be independent random variables such that
\phi_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})
and
\widetilde{\phi}_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})
have the same distribution. Applying Lemma
F.7
with
M_{h}=1
,
m\sim A_{n}(A_{n}^{(1)})^{-1}
, and
\tau\sim\beta(\underline{A}_{n2};A_{n})
, we have that for
\bm{\Delta}\in\{1,2\}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t>0}\left|\Prob\left(\left|\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\phi_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})\right|>t\right)-\Prob\left(\left|\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\widetilde{\phi}_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})\right|>t\right)\right|\lesssim\left({A_{n}\over A_{n}^{(1)}}\right)\beta(\underline{A}_{n2};A_{n})=o(1),

(C.3)

\displaystyle\sup_{t>0}\left|\Prob\left(\left|\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n2}}\phi_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})\right|>t\right)-\Prob\left(\left|\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n2}}\widetilde{\phi}_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})\right|>t\right)\right|\lesssim\left({A_{n}\over A_{n}^{(1)}}\right)\beta(\underline{A}_{n2};A_{n})=o(1).

(C.4)

These results imply that

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\phi_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\widetilde{\phi}_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})\right),\ \sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n2}}\phi_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})=O_{p}\left(\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n2}}\widetilde{\phi}_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})\right).

From (
C.3
) and (
C.4
), we have

\displaystyle\Prob\left(\max_{\bm{z}_{n}\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\left|\Phi_{n,1}(\bm{z}_{n})\right|>{C\over 2}\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\sqrt{{\log n\over A_{n}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq[\![\mathcal{S}_{n}]\!]\max_{\bm{z}_{n}\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\Prob\left(\left|\Phi_{n,1}(\bm{z}_{n})\right|>{C\over 2}\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\sqrt{{\log n\over A_{n}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\bm{\Delta}\in\{1,2\}^{d}}\widehat{Q}_{n1}(\bm{\Delta})+\sum_{\bm{\Delta}\in\{1,2\}^{d}}\widehat{Q}_{n2}(\bm{\Delta})+2^{d+1}[\![\mathcal{S}_{n}]\!]\left({A_{n}\over A_{n}^{(1)}}\right)\beta(\underline{A}_{n2};A_{n})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\bm{\Delta}\in\{1,2\}^{d}}\widehat{Q}_{n1}(\bm{\Delta})+\sum_{\bm{\Delta}\in\{1,2\}^{d}}\widehat{Q}_{n2}(\bm{\Delta})+\underbrace{\left({n^{d\eta_{1}}A_{n}\over A_{n}^{(1)}}\right)\beta(\underline{A}_{n2};A_{n})}_{=o(1)},

where

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{nj}(\bm{\Delta})

\displaystyle=[\![\mathcal{S}_{n}]\!]\max_{\bm{z}_{n}\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\Prob\left(\left|\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{nj}}\widetilde{\phi}_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z}_{n})\right|>{C\over 2^{d+2}}n\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\sqrt{{\log n\over A_{n}}}\right),\ j=1,2.

Now we restrict our attention to show that
\widehat{Q}_{n1}(\bm{\Delta})=o(1)
for
\bm{\Delta}\neq\bm{\Delta}_{0}
. The proofs for other cases are similar. Note that

\displaystyle\Prob\left(\left|\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\widetilde{\phi}_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})\right|>{C\over 2^{d+2}}n\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\sqrt{{\log n\over A_{n}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\Prob\left(\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\widetilde{\phi}_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})>{C\over 2^{d+2}}n\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\sqrt{{\log n\over A_{n}}}\right).

Observe that
\widetilde{\phi}_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})
are zero-mean independent random variables,

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{\phi}_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\phi_{11}}\zeta_{J}^{2}\lambda_{J}^{2}(\overline{A}_{n1})^{d-1}\overline{A}_{n2}nA_{n}^{-1}\tau_{n},\ a.s.\ (\text{from Lemma \ref{lem:number-summands}})

(C.5)

for some
C_{\phi_{11}}>0
and

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\E\left[\left(\widetilde{\phi}_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}n\E\left[\left|\psi_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}(\bm{z})\right|1\{\bm{S}_{1}\in\Gamma_{n}(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})\cap R_{n}\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\E\left[\left|\psi_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{i_{1}}\over A_{n}}\right)\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{i_{2}}\over A_{n}}\right)^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}(\bm{z})\right|\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad\times\left|\sigma_{\bm{e}}(\bm{S}_{i_{1}}-\bm{S}_{i_{2}})\right|1\{\bm{S}_{i_{1}},\bm{S}_{i_{2}}\in\Gamma_{n}(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})\cap R_{n}\}\right]

\displaystyle=n\E\left[\left|\psi_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}(\bm{z})\right|1\{\bm{S}_{1}\in\cup_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\Gamma_{n}(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})\cap R_{n}\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\E\left[\left|\psi_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{i_{1}}\over A_{n}}\right)\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{i_{2}}\over A_{n}}\right)^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}(\bm{z})\right|\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad\times\left|\sigma_{\bm{e}}(\bm{S}_{i_{1}}-\bm{S}_{i_{2}})\right|1\{\bm{S}_{i_{1}},\bm{S}_{i_{2}}\in\left(\cup_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\Gamma_{n}(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})\right)\cap R_{n}\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq n\E\left[\left|\psi_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}(\bm{z})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\quad+n(n-1)\E\left[\left|\psi_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{2}\over A_{n}}\right)^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}(\bm{z})\right|\left|\sigma_{\bm{e}}(\bm{S}_{1}-\bm{S}_{2})\right|\right]=:V_{n,1}+V_{n,2}.

For
V_{n,1}
, we have

\displaystyle V_{n,1}

\displaystyle=n\psi_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\E\left[\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)\psi_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)^{\prime}\right]\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}(\bm{z})=n\|\psi_{J}(\bm{z})\|^{2}\lesssim n\zeta_{J}^{2}\lambda_{J}^{2}.

For
V_{n,2}
, we have

\displaystyle V_{n,2}

\displaystyle\leq{n^{2}\over A_{n}^{2}}\int|\sigma_{\bm{e}}(\bm{s}_{1}-\bm{s}_{2})|\left|\psi_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}\left({\bm{s}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)\psi_{J}\left({\bm{s}_{2}\over A_{n}}\right)^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}(\bm{z})\right|g\left({\bm{s}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)g\left({\bm{s}_{2}\over A_{n}}\right)d\bm{s}_{1}d\bm{s}_{2}

\displaystyle=n^{2}\int|\sigma_{\bm{e}}(A_{n}(\bm{w}_{1}-\bm{w}_{2}))|\left|\psi_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}(\bm{w}_{1})\psi_{J}(\bm{w}_{2})^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}(\bm{z})\right|g(\bm{w}_{1})g(\bm{w}_{2})d\bm{w}_{1}d\bm{w}_{2}

\displaystyle={n^{2}\over A_{n}}\!\int_{\bar{R}_{n}}\!\!|\sigma_{\bm{e}}(\bm{x})|\!\left(\int\left|\psi_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}\!\left(\!{\bm{x}\over A_{n}}\!+\!\bm{w}_{2}\!\right)\!\psi_{J}(\bm{w}_{2})^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}(\bm{z})\right|g\!\left(\!{\bm{x}\over A_{n}}\!+\!\bm{w}_{2}\!\right)g(\bm{w}_{2})d\bm{w}_{2}\!\right)d\bm{x}

\displaystyle\lesssim{n^{2}\over A_{n}}\left(\int|\sigma_{\bm{e}}(\bm{x})|d\bm{x}\right)\left(\int\left|\psi_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}\left(\bm{w}_{2}\right)\psi_{J}(\bm{w}_{2})^{\prime}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}(\bm{z})\right|g(\bm{w}_{2})d\bm{w}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{n^{2}\zeta_{J}^{2}\lambda_{J}^{2}\over A_{n}}\left(\int|\sigma_{\bm{e}}(\bm{x})|d\bm{x}\right).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\E\left[\left(\widetilde{\phi}_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z})\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\phi_{12}}\zeta_{J}^{2}\lambda_{J}^{2}n^{2}A_{n}^{-1}

(C.6)

for some
C_{\phi_{12}}>0
. Then Lemma
F.8
, (
C.5
), and (
C.6
) yield that

\displaystyle\Prob\left(\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\widetilde{\phi}_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}(\bm{z}_{n})>{C\over 2^{d+2}}n\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\sqrt{{\log n\over A_{n}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-{E_{n0}\over E_{n1}+E_{n2}}\right),

where

E_{n0}\!=\!{C^{2}\zeta_{J}^{2}\lambda_{J}^{2}n^{2}\!\log n\over 2^{2d+5}A_{n}},\ E_{n1}\!=\!{C_{\phi_{11}}\zeta_{J}^{2}\lambda_{J}^{2}n^{2}\over A_{n}},\ E_{n2}\!=\!{CC_{\phi_{12}}\zeta_{J}^{3}\lambda_{J}^{3}(\overline{A}_{n1})^{d-1}\overline{A}_{n2}n^{2}\!\tau_{n}(\log n)^{1/2}\over 3\cdot 2^{d+2}A_{n}^{3/2}}.

From Assumption
2.4
(iii), we have
E_{n0}/E_{n1}\gtrsim\log n
and
E_{n0}/E_{n2}\gtrsim\log n
. This yields
\widehat{Q}_{n1}(\bm{\Delta})=o(1)
for large enough
C\geq 1
. Likewise, we can show that
\widehat{Q}_{nj}(\bm{\Delta})=o(1)
,
\bm{\Delta}\in\{1,2\}^{d}
,
j=1,2
. Therefore, we obtain
P_{n,211}=o(1)
for large enough
C\geq 1
.

(Step 4) Now we show (
A.1
). By the mean value theorem, for any
\bm{z},\bm{z}^{*}\in R_{0}
we have

\displaystyle|\dot{m}(\bm{z})-\dot{m}(\bm{z}^{*})|

\displaystyle=\left|(\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})-\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}^{*}))^{\prime}\left\{(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1})^{-1}-(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n)^{-}\right\}\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}M_{0}/n\right|

\displaystyle=\left|(\bm{z}-\bm{z}^{*})^{\prime}\nabla\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}^{**})^{\prime}\left\{(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1})^{-1}-(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n)^{-}\right\}\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}M_{0}/n\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}^{**})\|\|\bm{z}-\bm{z}^{*}\|\|\left\{(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1})^{-1}-(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n)^{-}\right\}|\|\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}M_{0}/n\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1/2}\|\|\nabla\psi_{J}(\bm{z}^{**})\|\|\bm{z}-\bm{z}^{*}\|\|\left\{(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1})^{-1}-(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n)^{-}\right\}\|\|\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}M_{0}/n\|

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\text{max}}(\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1/2})\|\nabla\psi_{J}(\bm{z}^{**})\|\|\bm{z}-\bm{z}^{*}\|\|\left\{(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1})^{-1}-(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n)^{-}\right\}\|\|\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}M_{0}/n\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\nabla}\lambda_{J}J^{\omega}\|\bm{z}-\bm{z}^{*}\|\|\left\{(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1})^{-1}-(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n)^{-}\right\}\|\|\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}M_{0}/n\|

for some
\bm{z}^{**}
in the segment between
\bm{z}
and
\bm{z}^{*}
and some finite constant
C_{\nabla}
which is independent of
\bm{z},\bm{z}^{*},n
, and
J
.

Observe that

\displaystyle\E\left[\left\|\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}M_{0}/n\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle={1\over n^{2}}\!\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\!\E\!\left[\psi^{\prime}_{J}\!\left({\bm{S}_{i_{1}}\over A_{n}}\right)\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\psi_{J}\!\left({\bm{S}_{i_{2}}\over A_{n}}\right)m_{0}\!\left({\bm{S}_{i_{1}}\over A_{n}}\right)m_{0}\!\left({\bm{S}_{i_{2}}\over A_{n}}\right)\right]\leq\left({1\over n}\!+\!1\right){\zeta_{J}^{2}\lambda_{J}^{2}\|m_{0}\|_{\infty}^{2}}.

Then, applying Markov’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}M_{0}/n\|=O_{p}\left(\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\|m_{0}\|_{\infty}\right).

(C.7)

From Lemmas
F.2
and
F.5
and Assumptions
2.4
(i), We have

\displaystyle\|(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1})^{-1}-(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n)^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\lesssim{1\over\varsigma_{J,n}^{-1}\lambda_{\text{min}}(\check{\Psi}_{J})-\varsigma_{J,n}^{-1}\lambda_{\text{max}}(\check{\Psi}_{J})\left\{\|(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1})-I_{J}\|+\|\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n-I_{J}\|\right\}}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left({1\over\varsigma_{J,n}^{-1}\lambda_{J}^{2}-\varsigma_{J,n}^{-1}\zeta_{J}^{2}(\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\sqrt{\log J/n})}\right)=O_{p}(\varsigma_{J,n}\lambda_{J}^{-2}).

(C.8)

Together with (
C.7
) and (
C.1
), we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\Prob\left(\!C_{\nabla}\!\left\|\left\{\!\!\left({\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}\over n}\!+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\!\!\right)^{-1}\!\!\!-\!\left({\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}\over n}\!\right)^{-}\!\right\}\right\|\!\left\|{\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}M_{0}\over n}\right\|\!>\bar{M}_{1}\right)\!=0

for any fixed
\bar{M}_{1}>0
since
\varsigma_{J,n}\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}^{-1}\lesssim\zeta_{J}^{2}\lambda_{J}^{-2}\sqrt{\log n/n}=o(1)
. Let
\mathcal{C}_{n}
denote the event on which
C_{\nabla}\|(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n\!+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}/n)^{-1}\!-(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n)^{-}\|\|\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}M_{0}/n\|\leq\bar{M}_{1}
and observe that
\Prob(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{c})=o(1)
. On
\mathcal{C}_{n}
, for any
C\geq 1
, a finite positive
\eta_{3}=\eta_{3}(C)
and
\eta_{4}=\eta_{4}(C)
can be chosen such that

\displaystyle C_{\nabla}\lambda_{J}J^{\omega}\|\bm{z}-\bm{z}^{*}\|\|(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}/n)^{-1}-(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n)^{-}\|\|\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}M_{0}/n\|\leq C\varsigma_{J,n}\zeta_{J}^{2}\|m_{0}\|_{\infty}

whenever
\|\bm{z}-\bm{z}^{*}\|\leq\eta_{4}n^{-\eta_{3}}
. Let
\mathcal{T}_{n}
be the smallest subset of
R_{0}
such that for each
\bm{z}\in R_{0}
there exists a
\bm{z}_{n}\in R_{0}
with
\|\bm{z}-\bm{z}_{n}\|\leq\eta_{4}n^{-\eta_{3}}
. For any
\bm{z}\in R_{0}
let
\bm{z}_{n}(\bm{z})
denote the
\bm{z}_{n}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}
nearest to
\bm{z}
in Euclidean distance. Then on
\mathcal{C}_{n}
, we have
|\dot{m}(\bm{z})-\dot{m}(\bm{z}_{n}(\bm{z}))|\leq C\varsigma_{J,n}\zeta_{J}^{2}\|m_{0}\|_{\infty}
for any
\bm{z}\in R_{0}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\Prob\left(\|\dot{m}\|_{\infty}\geq 4C\varsigma_{J,n}\zeta_{J}^{2}\|m_{0}\|_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Prob\left(\left\{\|\dot{m}\|_{\infty}\geq 4C\varsigma_{J,n}\zeta_{J}^{2}|m_{0}\|_{\infty}\right\}\cap\mathcal{C}_{n}\right)+\Prob(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq\Prob\left(\left\{\sup_{\bm{z}\in R_{0}}|\dot{m}(\bm{z})-\dot{m}(\bm{z}_{n}(\bm{z}))|\geq 2C\varsigma_{J,n}\zeta_{J}^{2}\|m_{0}\|_{\infty}\right\}\cap\mathcal{C}_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\Prob\left(\left\{\max_{\bm{z}_{n}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}}|\dot{m}(\bm{z}_{n})|\geq 2C\varsigma_{J,n}\zeta_{J}^{2}\|m_{0}\|_{\infty}\right\}\cap\mathcal{C}_{n}\right)+\Prob(\mathcal{C}_{n}^{c})

\displaystyle=\Prob\left(\left\{\max_{\bm{z}_{n}\in\mathcal{T}_{n}}|\dot{m}(\bm{z}_{n})|\geq 2C\varsigma_{J,n}\zeta_{J}^{2}\|m_{0}\|_{\infty}\right\}\cap\mathcal{C}_{n}\right)+o(1)=:P_{n,3}+o(1).

Since

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}_{n})^{\prime}\left\{(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}/n)^{-1}-(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n)^{-}\right\}\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}M_{0}/n\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\|(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}/n)^{-1}-(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n)^{-}\|\|\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}M_{0}/n\|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\varsigma_{J,n}\zeta_{J}^{2}\|m_{0}\|_{\infty}\right),

P_{n,3}
can be made arbitrarily small for large enough
C\geq 1
. Therefore, we complete the proof.

C.2.
Proof of Theorem
2.2

Let
\bm{z}=\bm{z}_{1}
. We prove

\displaystyle{\sqrt{A_{n}}(\widehat{m}(\bm{z})-m_{0}(\bm{z}))\over\sqrt{\Omega_{J}(\bm{z})}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}N(0,1).

For the multivariate case (i.e.,
L\geq 2
), the desired result follows from the Camér-Wold device.

Note that

\displaystyle{\sqrt{A_{n}}(\widehat{m}(\bm{z})-m_{0}(\bm{z}))\over\sqrt{\Omega_{J}(\bm{z})}}

\displaystyle={\sqrt{A_{n}}(\widehat{m}(\bm{z})-\bar{m}(\bm{z}))\over\sqrt{\Omega_{J}(\bm{z})}}+{\sqrt{A_{n}}(\bar{m}(\bm{z})-\widetilde{m}(\bm{z}))\over\sqrt{\Omega_{J}(\bm{z})}}+{\sqrt{A_{n}}(\widetilde{m}(\bm{z})-m_{0}(\bm{z}))\over\sqrt{\Omega_{J}(\bm{z})}}.

Observe that

\displaystyle\Omega_{J}(\bm{z})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\left\{\kappa\int(\eta^{2}(\bm{v})+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}(\bm{v}))\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{v})\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{v})^{\prime}g(\bm{v})d\bm{v}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad+\left(\int\eta^{2}(\bm{v})\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{v})\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{v})^{\prime}g^{2}(\bm{v})d\bm{v}\right)\left(\int\sigma_{\bm{e}}(\bm{x})d\bm{x}\right)\right\}\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})

\displaystyle\sim\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\left\{\int\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{v})\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{v})^{\prime}g(\bm{v})d\bm{v}\right\}\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})=\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\|^{2}.

Condition (b) yields that

\left|{\sqrt{A_{n}}(\widetilde{m}(\bm{z})-m_{0}(\bm{z}))\over\sqrt{\Omega_{J}(\bm{z})}}\right|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{A_{n}}\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\|^{-1}\inf_{m\in\Psi_{J}}\|m_{0}-m\|_{\infty}\right)=o_{p}(1).

Condition (c) yields that

\displaystyle\left|{\sqrt{A_{n}}(\bar{m}(\bm{z})-\widetilde{m}(\bm{z}))\over\sqrt{\Omega_{J}(\bm{z})}}\right|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{A_{n}}\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\|^{-1}\|\bar{m}-\widetilde{m}\|_{\infty}\right)=O_{p}(\sqrt{A_{n}}\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\|^{-1}\varsigma_{J,n}\zeta_{J}^{2})=o_{p}(1).

Then it is sufficient to show that

\displaystyle{\sqrt{A_{n}}(\widehat{m}(\bm{z})-\bar{m}(\bm{z}))\over\sqrt{\Omega_{J}(\bm{z})}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}N(0,1).

(C.9)

We show (
C.9
) in several steps.

(Step 1) In this step, we give an overview of the proof of (
C.9
). Recall that

\displaystyle{\sqrt{A_{n}}\left(\widehat{m}(\bm{z})-\bar{m}(\bm{z})\right)\over\sqrt{\Omega_{J}(\bm{z}})}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{{A_{n}\over\Omega_{J}(\bm{z})}}\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1})^{-1}\left(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}v_{n}\over n\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{{A_{n}\over\Omega_{J}(\bm{z})}}\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\left(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}v_{n}\over n\right)+\sqrt{{A_{n}\over\Omega_{J}(\bm{z})}}\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})^{\prime}\left\{(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1})^{-1}-I_{J}\right\}\left(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}v_{n}\over n\right)

\displaystyle=:K_{n,1}+K_{n,2}.

For
K_{n,2}
, from Lemma
F.2
and (
A.5
), we have

\displaystyle|K_{n,2}|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{A_{n}}\left\|{\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\over\sqrt{\Omega_{J}(\bm{z})}}\right\|\|(\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}/n+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1})^{-1}-I_{J}\|\left\|{\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}v_{n}\over n}\right\|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{A_{n}}\times O_{p}\left(\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\sqrt{\log J\over n}+\varsigma_{J,n}\lambda_{J}^{2}\right)\times O_{p}\left({\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}\over\sqrt{A_{n}}}\right)=o_{p}(1).

(C.10)

For
K_{n,1}
, note that

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}_{n,1}

\displaystyle:={\sqrt{n^{2}A_{n}^{-1}\Omega_{J}(\bm{z})}\over\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\|}K_{n,1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left({\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\over\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\|}\right)^{\prime}\widetilde{\psi}_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}}\right)(e_{n,i}+\varepsilon_{n,i})=:\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}}\right)(e_{n,i}+\varepsilon_{n,i})

and
\|k_{J}\|_{\infty}\leq\zeta_{J}\lambda_{J}
. Decompose

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}_{n,1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\!\!\!\!k_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta}_{0})}\!+\!\sum_{\bm{\Delta}\neq\bm{\Delta}_{0}}\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\!\!\!\!k_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}\!+\!\sum_{\bm{\Delta}\in\{1,2\}^{d}}\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n2}}\!\!\!\!k_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}=:K_{n,11}+K_{n,12}+K_{n,13},

where
k_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{J}(\bm{S}_{i}/A_{n})(e_{n,i}+\varepsilon_{n,i})1\{\bm{S}_{i}\in\Gamma_{n}(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})\cap R_{n}\}
.

Note that for
\bm{\ell}_{1},\bm{\ell}_{2}\in L_{n1}
with
\bm{\ell}_{1}\neq\bm{\ell}_{2}
,

\displaystyle d\left(\Gamma_{n}(\bm{\ell}_{1};\bm{\Delta}_{0}),\Gamma_{n}(\bm{\ell}_{2};\bm{\Delta}_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\geq\max\{|\bm{\ell}_{1}-\bm{\ell}_{2}|-d,0\}\underline{A}_{n3}+\underline{A}_{n2},

(C.11)

where
\underline{A}_{n3}=\min_{1\leq j\leq d}A_{n3,j}
,
A_{n3,j}=A_{n1,j}+A_{n2,j}
, and
\underline{A}_{n2}=\min_{1\leq j\leq d}A_{n2,j}
.

Hence, by the Volkonskii-Rozanov inequality (cf. Proposition 2.6 in
Fan and Yao, 2003
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\E[\exp(\mathrm{i}uK_{n,11})]-\prod_{\ell\in L_{n1}}\E[\exp(\mathrm{i}uk_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta}_{0})})]\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim{A_{n}\over A_{n}^{(1)}}\alpha(\underline{A}_{n2};A_{n}).

(C.12)

From Lyapounov’s central limit theorem, it is sufficient to verify the following conditions to show (
C.9
): As
n,J\to\infty
,

\displaystyle{A_{n}\over n^{2}}\E[\widetilde{K}_{n,1}^{2}]=\left({\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\over\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\|}\right)^{\prime}G_{J}\left({\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\over\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\|}\right)(1+o(1)),

(C.13)

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\E[(k_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta}_{0})})^{2}]-\E[\widetilde{K}_{n,1}^{2}]=o\left(n^{2}A_{n}^{-1}\right),

(C.14)

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\E[(k_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta}_{0})})^{4}]=o\left(n^{4}A_{n}^{-2}\right),

(C.15)

\displaystyle\Var(K_{n,12})=o\left(n^{2}A_{n}^{-1}\right),

(C.16)

\displaystyle\Var(K_{n,13})=o\left(n^{2}A_{n}^{-1}\right).

(C.17)

We show (
C.13
) in Step 2, (
C.15
) in Step 3, (
C.16
) and (
C.17
) in Step 4, and (
C.14
) in Step 5.

(Step 2) Now we show (
C.13
). Observe that

\displaystyle\E[\widetilde{K}_{n,1}^{2}]

\displaystyle=n\E\left[k_{J}^{2}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)(e_{n,1}^{2}+\varepsilon_{n,1}^{2})\right]+n(n-1)\E\left[k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{2}\over A_{n}}\right)e_{n,1}e_{n,2}\right]

\displaystyle={n\over A_{n}}\int k_{J}^{2}\left({\bm{s}\over A_{n}}\right)\left\{\eta^{2}\left({\bm{s}\over A_{n}}\right)+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}\left({\bm{s}\over A_{n}}\right)\right\}g\left({\bm{s}\over A_{n}}\right)d\bm{s}

\displaystyle\quad+{n(n-1)\over A_{n}^{2}}\!\!\int\!k_{J}\!\left({\bm{s}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)k_{J}\!\left({\bm{s}_{2}\over A_{n}}\right)\eta\!\left({\bm{s}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)\eta\!\left({\bm{s}_{2}\over A_{n}}\right)\sigma_{\bm{e}}(\bm{s}_{1}-\bm{s}_{2})g\!\left({\bm{s}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)g\!\left({\bm{s}_{2}\over A_{n}}\right)d\bm{s}_{1}d\bm{s}_{2}

\displaystyle=:\widetilde{K}_{n,11}+\widetilde{K}_{n,12}.

For
\widetilde{K}_{n,11}
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}_{n,11}

\displaystyle=n\int k_{J}^{2}(\bm{w})\left\{\eta^{2}(\bm{w})+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}(\bm{w})\right\}g(\bm{w})d\bm{w}

\displaystyle=n\left({\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\over\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\|}\right)^{\prime}\left(\int\left\{\eta^{2}(\bm{w})+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}(\bm{w})\right\}\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{w})\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{w})^{\prime}g(\bm{w})d\bm{w}\right)\left({\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\over\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\|}\right).

(C.18)

For
K_{n,12}
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}_{n,12}

\displaystyle=n(n-1)\int k_{J}(\bm{w}_{1})k_{J}(\bm{w}_{2})\eta(\bm{w}_{1})\eta(\bm{w}_{2})\sigma_{\bm{e}}(A_{n}(\bm{w}_{1}-\bm{w}_{2}))g(\bm{w}_{1})g(\bm{w}_{2})d\bm{w}_{1}d\bm{w}_{2}

\displaystyle={n(n-1)\over A_{n}}\!\!\int_{\bar{R}_{n}}\!\!\!\sigma_{\bm{e}}(\bm{x})\!\!\left(\int\!\!k_{J}\!\!\left({\bm{x}\over A_{n}}\!+\!\bm{w}_{2}\right)\!k_{J}(\bm{w}_{2})\eta\!\left({\bm{x}\over A_{n}}\!+\!\bm{w}_{2}\right)\eta(\bm{w}_{2})g\!\left({\bm{x}\over A_{n}}\!+\!\bm{w}_{2}\right)\!g(\bm{w}_{2})d\bm{w}_{2}\right)d\bm{x}

where
\bar{R}_{n}=\{\bm{x}=\bm{x}_{1}-\bm{x}_{2}:\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2}\in R_{n}\}
. Then as
n,J\to\infty
, we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}_{n,12}

\displaystyle={n^{2}\over A_{n}}\left(\int k_{J}^{2}(\bm{w})\eta^{2}(\bm{w})g^{2}(\bm{w})d\bm{w}\right)\left(\int\sigma_{\bm{e}}(\bm{x})d\bm{x}\right)(1+o(1))

\displaystyle={n^{2}\over A_{n}}\left({\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\over\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\|}\right)^{\prime}\left(\int\eta^{2}(\bm{w})\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{w})\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{w})^{\prime}g^{2}(\bm{w})d\bm{w}\right)\left(\int\sigma_{\bm{e}}(\bm{x})d\bm{x}\right)\left({\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\over\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\|}\right)(1+o(1)).

(C.19)

Then (
C.2
) and (
C.19
) yield (
C.13
).

(Step 3) Now we show (
C.15
). Define
I_{n}(\bm{\ell})\!=\!\{\bm{i}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{d}\!:\bm{i}\!+\!(-1/2,1/2]^{d}\!\subset\!\Gamma_{n}(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta}_{0})\}
for
\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}
and
\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{i})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{J}(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n})(e_{n,i}+\varepsilon_{n,i})1\{\bm{S}_{i}\in[\bm{i}+(-1/2,1/2]^{d}]\cap R_{n}\}
.

Observe that

\displaystyle\E[(k_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta}_{0})})^{4}]=\E\left[\left(\sum_{\bm{i}\in I_{n}(\bm{\ell})}\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{i})\right)^{4}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{i}\in I_{n}(\bm{\ell})}\E\left[\widetilde{K}_{n}^{4}(\bm{i})\right]+\sum_{\bm{i},\bm{j}\in I_{n}(\bm{\ell}),\bm{i}\neq\bm{j}}\E\left[\widetilde{K}_{n}^{3}(\bm{i})\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{j})\right]+\sum_{\bm{i},\bm{j}\in I_{n}(\bm{\ell}),\bm{i}\neq\bm{j}}\E\left[\widetilde{K}_{n}^{2}(\bm{i})\widetilde{K}_{n}^{2}(\bm{j})\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k}\in I_{n}(\bm{\ell}),\bm{i}\neq\bm{j}\neq\bm{k}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\E\!\left[\widetilde{K}_{n}^{2}(\bm{i})\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{j})\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{k})\right]+\sum_{\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p}\in I_{n}(\bm{\ell}),\bm{i}\neq\bm{j}\neq\bm{k}\neq\bm{p}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\E\!\left[\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{i})\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{j})\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{k})\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{p})\right]

\displaystyle=:Q_{n1}+Q_{n2}+Q_{n3}+Q_{n4}+Q_{n5}.

For
Q_{n1}
, we have

\displaystyle\E[\widetilde{K}_{n}^{4}(\bm{i})]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}=1}^{n}\E\left[\prod_{k=1}^{4}k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{j_{k}}\over A_{n}}\right)1\{\bm{S}_{j_{k}}\in[\bm{i}+(-1/2,1/2]^{d}]\cap R_{n}\}(e_{n,j_{k}}+\varepsilon_{n,j_{k}})\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}=1}^{n}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\E\left[\prod_{k=1}^{4}k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{j_{k}}\over A_{n}}\right)1\{\bm{S}_{j_{k}}\!\!\in\![\bm{i}\!+\!(-1/2,1/2]^{d}]\cap R_{n}\}\eta\!\left(\!{\bm{S}_{j_{k}}\over A_{n}}\!\right)e(\bm{S}_{j_{k}})\!\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3},j_{4}=1}^{n}\!\!\!\!\!\!\E\left[\prod_{k=1}^{4}k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{j_{k}}\over A_{n}}\right)1\{\bm{S}_{j_{k}}\!\!\in\![\bm{i}\!+\!(-1/2,1/2]^{d}]\!\cap\!R_{n}\}\sigma_{\varepsilon}\!\left(\!{\bm{S}_{j_{k}}\over A_{n}}\!\right)\varepsilon_{j_{k}}\!\right]=:Q_{n11}+Q_{n12}.

For
Q_{n11}
, we have

\displaystyle Q_{n11}

\displaystyle\lesssim n\E\left[\left|k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)\right|^{4}1\{\bm{S}_{1}\in[\bm{i}+(-1/2,1/2]^{d}]\cap R_{n}\}\eta^{4}(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n})\right]

\displaystyle\quad+n^{2}\E\left[\left|k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)\right|^{3}1\{\bm{S}_{1}\in[\bm{i}+(-1/2,1/2]^{d}]\cap R_{n}\}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\times\left|k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{j_{k}}\over A_{n}}\right)\right|1\{\bm{S}_{2}\in[\bm{i}+(-1/2,1/2]^{d}]\cap R_{n}\}\eta^{3}(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n})\eta(\bm{S}_{2}/A_{n})\right]

\displaystyle\quad+n^{2}\E\left[\prod_{t=1}^{2}\left|k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{t}\over A_{n}}\right)\right|^{2}\!\!1\{\bm{S}_{t}\in[\bm{i}+(-1/2,1/2]^{d}]\cap R_{n}\}\eta^{2}(\bm{S}_{t}/A_{n})\right]

\displaystyle\quad+n^{3}\E\left[\left|k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{1}\over A_{n}}\right)\right|^{2}1\{\bm{S}_{1}\in[\bm{i}+(-1/2,1/2]^{d}]\cap R_{n}\}\eta^{2}(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n})\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\times\prod_{t=2}^{3}\left|k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{t}\over A_{n}}\right)\right|1\{\bm{S}_{t}\in[\bm{i}+(-1/2,1/2]^{d}]\cap R_{n}\}\eta(\bm{S}_{t}/A_{n})\right]

\displaystyle\quad+n^{4}\E\left[\prod_{t=1}^{4}\left|k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{t}\over A_{n}}\right)\right|1\{\bm{S}_{t}\in[\bm{i}+(-1/2,1/2]^{d}]\cap R_{n}\}\eta(\bm{S}_{t}/A_{n})\right]

\displaystyle=:Q_{n111}+Q_{n112}+Q_{n113}+Q_{n114}.

For
Q_{n111}
, we have

\displaystyle Q_{n111}

\displaystyle=nA_{n}^{-1}\int\left|k_{J}\left({\bm{s}\over A_{n}}\right)\right|^{4}1\{\bm{s}\in[\bm{i}+(-1/2,1/2]^{d}]\cap R_{n}\}\eta^{4}(\bm{s}/A_{n})g(\bm{s}/A_{n})d\bm{s}

\displaystyle\leq nA_{n}^{-1}\zeta_{J}^{4}\lambda_{J}^{4}\int 1\{\bm{s}\in R_{n}\}\eta^{4}(\bm{s}/A_{n})g(\bm{s}/A_{n})d\bm{s}=O\left(nA_{n}^{-1}\zeta_{J}^{4}\lambda_{J}^{4}\right).

Likewise,
Q_{n112}=O(n^{2}A_{n}^{-2}\zeta_{J}^{4}\lambda_{J}^{4})
,
Q_{n113}=O(n^{3}A_{n}^{-3}\zeta_{J}^{4}\lambda_{J}^{4})
, and
Q_{n114}=O(n^{4}A_{n}^{-4}\zeta_{J}^{4}\lambda_{J}^{4})
. Then we have
Q_{n11}=O(n^{4}A_{n}^{-4}\zeta_{J}^{4}\lambda_{J}^{4})
. We can also show that
Q_{n12}=O(n^{4}A_{n}^{-4}\zeta_{J}^{4}\lambda_{J}^{4})
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle Q_{n1}

\displaystyle\lesssim[\![I_{n}(\bm{\ell})]\!]n^{4}A_{n}^{-4}\zeta_{J}^{4}\lambda_{J}^{4}\lesssim A_{n}^{(1)}(nA_{n}^{-1})^{4}\zeta_{J}^{4}\lambda_{J}^{4}.

(C.20)

For
Q_{n2}
, by the
\alpha
-mixing property of
\bm{e}
and Proposition 2.5 in
Fan and Yao, 2003
, we have

\displaystyle Q_{n2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k=1}^{\overline{A}_{n1}}\sum_{\bm{i},\bm{j}\in I_{n}(\bm{\ell}),|\bm{i}-\bm{j}|=k}\alpha^{1-4/q}(\max\{k-d,0\};1)\E[|\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{i})|^{q}]^{3/q}\E[|\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{j})|^{q}]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{n}^{(1)}(nA_{n}^{-1})^{4}\zeta_{J}^{4}\lambda_{J}^{4}\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{\overline{A}_{n1}}k^{d-1}\alpha_{1}^{1-4/q}(k)\right).

(C.21)

where
\overline{A}_{n1}=\max_{1\leq j\leq d}A_{n1,j}
. Likewise,

\displaystyle Q_{n3}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{n}^{(1)}(nA_{n}^{-1})^{4}\zeta_{J}^{4}\lambda_{J}^{4}\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{\overline{A}_{n1}}k^{d-1}\alpha_{1}^{1-4/q}(k)\right).

(C.22)

Now we evaluate
Q_{n4}
and
Q_{n5}
. For distinct indices
\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p}\in I_{n}(\bm{\ell})
, let

\displaystyle d_{1}(\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k})

\displaystyle=\max\{d(\{\bm{i}\},\{\bm{j},\bm{k}\}),d(\{\bm{k}\},\{\bm{i},\bm{j}\})\},

\displaystyle d_{2}(\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p})

\displaystyle=\max\{d(F,\{\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p}\}):F\subset\{\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p}\},[\![F]\!]=1\},

\displaystyle d_{3}(\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p})

\displaystyle=\max\{d(F,\{\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p}\}):F\subset\{\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p}\},[\![F]\!]=2\}.

Here,
d_{1}
denotes the maximal gap in the set of integer-indices
\{\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k}\}
from either
\bm{j}
or
\bm{k}
which corresponds to
\E\left[\widetilde{K}_{n}^{2}(\bm{i})\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{j})\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{k})\right]
. Similarly,
d_{2}
and
d_{3}
are the maximal gap in the index set
\{\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p}\}
from any of its single index-subsets or two-index subsets, respectively. Applying the argument in the proof of Lemma 4.1 of
Lahiri, 1999
, for any given values
1\leq d_{01},d_{02},d_{03}<[\![I_{n}(\bm{\ell})]\!]
, we have

\displaystyle[\![\{(\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k})\in I_{n}^{3}(\bm{\ell}):\bm{i}\neq\bm{j}\neq\bm{k}\ \text{and}\ d_{1}(\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k})=d_{01}\}]\!]\lesssim d_{01}^{2d-1}[\![I_{n}(\bm{\ell})]\!],

(C.23)

\displaystyle[\![\{(\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p})\in I_{n}^{4}(\bm{\ell}):\bm{i}\neq\bm{j}\neq\bm{k}\neq\bm{p},\ d_{2}(\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p})=d_{02},\ \text{and}\ d_{3}(\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p})=d_{03}\}]\!]

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim(d_{02}+d_{03})^{3d-1}[\![I_{n}(\bm{\ell})]\!].

(C.24)

For
Q_{n4}
, by (
C.23
) and applying the same argument to show (
C.21
), we have

\displaystyle Q_{n4}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{n}^{(1)}\sum_{k=1}^{\overline{A}_{n1}}k^{2d-1}\alpha^{1-4/q}(\max\{k-d,0\};2)\E[|\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{i})|^{q}]^{2/q}\E[|\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{j})|^{q}]^{1/q}\E[|\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{k})|^{q}]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{n}^{(1)}(nA_{n}^{-1})^{4}\zeta_{J}^{4}\lambda_{J}^{4}\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{\overline{A}_{n1}}k^{2d-1}\alpha_{1}^{1-4/q}(k)\right).

(C.25)

Define

\displaystyle I_{n1}(\bm{\ell})

\displaystyle=\{(\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p})\in I_{n}^{4}(\bm{\ell}):\bm{i}\neq\bm{j}\neq\bm{k}\neq\bm{p},\ d_{2}(\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p})\geq d_{3}(\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p})\},

\displaystyle I_{n2}(\bm{\ell})

\displaystyle=\{(\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p})\in I_{n}^{4}(\bm{\ell}):\bm{i}\neq\bm{j}\neq\bm{k}\neq\bm{p},\ d_{2}(\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p})<d_{3}(\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p})\}.

For
Q_{n5}
, by (
C.24
) and applying the same argument to show (
C.21
), we have

\displaystyle Q_{n5}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p})\in I_{n1}(\bm{\ell})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\E\left[\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{i})\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{j})\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{k})\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{p})\right]+\sum_{(\bm{i},\bm{j},\bm{k},\bm{p})\in I_{n2}(\bm{\ell})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\E\left[\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{i})\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{j})\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{k})\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{p})\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{n}^{(1)}\sum_{k=1}^{\overline{A}_{n1}}k^{3d-1}\alpha^{1-4/q}(\max\{k-d,0\};3)

\displaystyle\quad\times\E[|\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{i})|^{q}]^{1/q}\E[|\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{j})|^{q}]^{1/q}\E[|\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{k})|^{q}]^{1/q}\E[|\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{p})|^{q}]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\sum_{\bm{i},\bm{j}\in I_{n}(\bm{\ell}),\bm{i}\neq\bm{j}}\left|\E[\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{i})\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{j})]\right|\right)^{2}+A_{n}^{(1)}\sum_{k=1}^{\overline{A}_{n1}}k^{3d-1}\alpha^{1-4/q}(\max\{k-d,0\};2)

\displaystyle\quad\times\E[|\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{i})|^{q}]^{1/q}\E[|\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{j})|^{q}]^{1/q}\E[|\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{k})|^{q}]^{1/q}\E[|\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{p})|^{q}]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\lesssim A_{n}^{(1)}(\overline{A}_{n1})^{d}(nA_{n}^{-1})^{4}\zeta_{J}^{4}\lambda_{J}^{4}\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{\overline{A}_{n1}}k^{2d-1}\alpha_{1}^{1-4/q}(k)\right).

(C.26)

Combining (
C.20
), (
C.21
), (
C.22
), (
C.25
), and (
C.26
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\E[\widetilde{K}_{n}^{4}(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta}_{0})]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\E\left[\left(\sum_{\bm{i}\in I_{n}(\bm{\ell})}\widetilde{K}_{n}(\bm{i})\right)^{4}\right]\lesssim[\![L_{n1}]\!]A_{n}^{(1)}(\overline{A}_{n1})^{d}(nA_{n}^{-1})^{4}\zeta_{J}^{4}\lambda_{J}^{4}\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{\overline{A}_{n1}}k^{2d-1}\alpha_{1}^{1-4/q}(k)\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim{A_{n}\over A_{n}^{(1)}}A_{n}^{(1)}(\overline{A}_{n1})^{d}(nA_{n}^{-1})^{4}\zeta_{J}^{4}\lambda_{J}^{4}\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{\overline{A}_{n1}}k^{2d-1}\alpha_{1}^{1-4/q}(k)\right)=o\left(n^{4}A_{n}^{-2}\right).

(Step 4) Now we show (
C.16
) and (
C.17
). Define

\displaystyle J_{n}

\displaystyle=\{\bm{i}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}:(\bm{i}+(-1/2,1/2]^{d})\cap R_{n}\neq\emptyset\},\ J_{n1}=\cup_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}I_{n}(\bm{\ell}),

\displaystyle J_{n2}

\displaystyle=\{\bm{i}\in J_{n}\!:\!\bm{i}+(-1/2,1/2]^{d}\!\subset\!\Gamma_{n}(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta})\ \text{for some}\ \bm{\ell}\in L_{n1},\bm{\Delta}\!\neq\!\bm{\Delta}_{0}\},\ J_{n3}=J_{n}\backslash(J_{n1}\!\cup\!J_{n2}).

Note that
[\![J_{n2}]\!]\lesssim(\overline{A}_{n1})^{d-1}\overline{A}_{n2}A_{n}(A_{n}^{(1)})^{-1}
and
[\![J_{n3}]\!]\lesssim A_{n}^{(1)}\left({\overline{A}_{n}\over\underline{A}_{n1}}\right)^{d-1}
. Then, applying the same argument to show (
C.21
), we have

\displaystyle\Var(K_{n,12})

\displaystyle\lesssim[\![J_{n2}]\!](nA_{n}^{-1})^{2}\zeta_{J}^{2}\lambda_{J}^{2}\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{\overline{A}_{n1}}k^{d-1}\alpha_{1}^{1-2/q}(k)\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim{(\overline{A}_{n1})^{d-1}\overline{A}_{n2}\over A_{n}^{(1)}}n^{2}A_{n}^{-1}\zeta_{J}^{2}\lambda_{J}^{2}\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{\overline{A}_{n1}}k^{d-1}\alpha_{1}^{1-2/q}(k)\right)=o\left(n^{2}A_{n}^{-1}\right).

\displaystyle\Var(K_{n,13})

\displaystyle\lesssim[\![J_{n3}]\!](nA_{n}^{-1})^{2}\zeta_{J}^{2}\lambda_{J}^{2}\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{\overline{A}_{n1}}k^{d-1}\alpha_{1}^{1-2/q}(k)\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\!{A_{n}^{(1)}\over A_{n}}\left({\overline{A}_{n}\over\underline{A}_{n1}}\right)^{d-1}n^{2}A_{n}^{-1}\zeta_{J}^{2}\lambda_{J}^{2}\!\left(\!\!1\!+\!\sum_{k=1}^{\overline{A}_{n1}}k^{d-1}\alpha_{1}^{1-2/q}(k)\!\!\right)=o\left(n^{2}A_{n}^{-1}\right).

(Step 5) Now we show (
C.14
). By (
C.16
) and (
C.17
), we have for sufficiently large
n
,

\displaystyle\E[K_{n,11}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\E[\{\widetilde{K}_{n,1}-(K_{n,12}+K_{n,13})\}^{2}]\leq 2\left(\E[\widetilde{K}_{n,1}^{2}]+\E[(\widetilde{K}_{n,12}+\widetilde{K}_{n,13})^{2}]\right)\leq 4\E[\widetilde{K}_{n,1}^{2}].

Thus, by (
C.11
), (
C.16
), and (
C.17
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\E[(k_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta}_{0})})^{2}]-\E[\widetilde{K}_{n,1}^{2}]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{\bm{\ell}\in L_{n1}}\E[(k_{1}^{(\bm{\ell};\bm{\Delta}_{0})})^{2}]-\E[K_{n,11}^{2}]\right|+2\E[(K_{n,12}+K_{n,13})^{2}]^{1/2}\E[K_{n,11}^{2}]^{1/2}+\E[(K_{n,12}+K_{n,13})^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(A_{n}^{(1)}nA_{n}^{-1}\right)^{2}\zeta_{J}^{2}\lambda_{J}^{2}\!\!\sum_{\bm{\ell}_{1}\neq\bm{\ell}_{2}}\!\!\alpha^{1-2/q}(\max\{|\bm{\ell}_{1}\!-\!\bm{\ell}_{2}|-d,0\}\underline{A}_{n3}\!+\!\underline{A}_{n2};A_{n}^{(1)})\!+\!o\left(n^{2}A_{n}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(A_{n}^{(1)}nA_{n}^{-1}\right)^{2}\zeta_{J}^{2}\lambda_{J}^{2}A_{n}(A_{n}^{(1)})^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\alpha^{1-2/q}(\underline{A}_{n2};A_{n}^{(1)})+\sum_{k=1}^{\overline{A}_{n}/\underline{A}_{n1}}k^{d-1}\alpha^{1-2/q}(\max\{k-d,0\}\underline{A}_{n3}+\underline{A}_{n2};A_{n}^{(1)})\right)+o\left(n^{2}A_{n}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=o\left(n^{2}A_{n}^{-1}\right).

C.3.
Proof of Proposition
2.3

Note that
\widehat{\Omega}_{J}(\bm{z}_{1},\bm{z}_{2})=\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}_{1})^{\prime}\widetilde{G}_{J}\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}_{2})
where

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{J}

\displaystyle={A_{n}\over n^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\left({\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}\over n}+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\right)^{-1}\widetilde{\psi}_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}}\right)\widetilde{\psi}_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{j}\over A_{n}}\right)^{\prime}\left({\widetilde{\Psi}^{\prime}_{J,n}\widetilde{\Psi}_{J,n}\over n}+\varsigma_{J,n}\check{\Psi}_{J}^{-1}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\left(Y(\bm{S}_{i})-\widehat{m}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}}\right)\!\right)\left(Y(\bm{S}_{j})-\widehat{m}\left({\bm{S}_{j}\over A_{n}}\right)\!\right)\bar{K}_{b}(\bm{S}_{i}-\bm{S}_{j}).

Then it suffices to show that as
n,J\to\infty
,

\displaystyle W_{n,J}(\bm{z}_{1},\bm{z}_{2})

\displaystyle:=\left({\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}_{1})\over\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}_{1})\|}\right)^{\prime}\widetilde{G}_{J}\left({\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}_{2})\over\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}_{2})\|}\right)=\left({\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}_{1})\over\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}_{1})\|}\right)^{\prime}G_{J}\left({\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}_{2})\over\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z}_{2})\|}\right)+o_{p}(1).

(C.27)

Now we restrict our attention to the case
\bm{z}_{1}=\bm{z}_{2}=\bm{z}
. The proofs for other cases are similar. Define
W_{n,J}(\bm{z}):=W_{n,J}(\bm{z},\bm{z})
. Applying Theorem
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle W_{n,J}(\bm{z})

\displaystyle={A_{n}\over n^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}}\right)k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{j}\over A_{n}}\right)\bar{K}_{b}(\bm{S}_{i}-\bm{S}_{j})(e_{n,i}+\varepsilon_{n,i})(e_{n,j}+\varepsilon_{n,j})+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle={A_{n}\over n^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}}\right)k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{j}\over A_{n}}\right)\bar{K}_{b}(\bm{S}_{i}-\bm{S}_{j})e_{n,i}e_{n,j}

\displaystyle\quad+{2A_{n}\over n^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}}\right)k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{j}\over A_{n}}\right)\bar{K}_{b}(\bm{S}_{i}-\bm{S}_{j})e_{n,i}\varepsilon_{n,j}

\displaystyle\quad+{A_{n}\over n^{2}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}k_{J}\!\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}}\right)k_{J}\!\left({\bm{S}_{j}\over A_{n}}\right)\bar{K}_{b}(\bm{S}_{i}-\bm{S}_{j})\varepsilon_{n,i}\varepsilon_{n,j}+o_{p}(1)=:W_{n,1}+W_{n,2}+W_{n,3}+o_{p}(1).

For
W_{n,3}
, observe that

\displaystyle W_{n,3}

\displaystyle={A_{n}\over n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{J}^{2}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}}\right)\varepsilon_{n,i}^{2}+{A_{n}\over n^{2}}\sum_{i\neq j}^{n}k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{i}\over A_{n}}\right)k_{J}\left({\bm{S}_{j}\over A_{n}}\right)\bar{K}_{b}(\bm{S}_{i}-\bm{S}_{j})\varepsilon_{n,i}\varepsilon_{n,j}=:W_{n,31}+W_{n,32}.

For
W_{n,31}
, we have

\displaystyle\E[W_{n,31}]

\displaystyle={A_{n}\over n}\int k_{J}^{2}(\bm{x})\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}(\bm{x}/A_{n})A_{n}^{-1}g(\bm{x}/A_{n})d\bm{x}={A_{n}\over n}\int k_{J}^{2}(\bm{v})\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}(\bm{v})g(\bm{v})d\bm{v}

\displaystyle=\kappa\left(\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\over\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\|\right)^{\prime}\left(\int\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}(\bm{v})\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{v})\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{v})^{\prime}g(\bm{v})d\bm{v}\right)\left(\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\over\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\|\right)^{\prime}(1+o(1)),

\displaystyle\Var(W_{n,31})

\displaystyle=\left({A_{n}\over n^{2}}\right)^{2}n\Var(k_{J}^{2}(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n})\varepsilon_{n,1}^{2})\leq\left({A_{n}\over n^{2}}\right)^{2}nE[k_{J}^{4}(\bm{S}_{1}/A_{n})\varepsilon_{n,1}^{4}]\lesssim{A_{n}^{2}\over n^{2}}{J^{2}\over n}=o(1).

Then we have

\displaystyle W_{n,31}=\kappa\left(\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\over\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\|\right)^{\prime}\left(\int\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}(\bm{v})\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{v})\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{v})^{\prime}g(\bm{v})d\bm{v}\right)\left(\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\over\|\widetilde{\psi}_{J}(\bm{z})\|\right)^{\prime}+o_{p}(1).

For
W_{n,32}
, applying similar arguments in the proof of Theorem
2.2
, we have
\E[W_{n,32}]=0
and

\displaystyle\left({A_{n}\over n^{2}}\right)^{-2}\E[W_{n,32}^{2}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\neq j,k\neq\ell}\!\!\!\!\!\E\!\left[k_{J}(\bm{S}_{i}/A_{n})k_{J}(\bm{S}_{j}/A_{n})\bar{K}_{b}(\bm{S}_{i}-\bm{S}_{j})k_{J}(\bm{S}_{k}/A_{n})k_{J}(\bm{S}_{\ell}/A_{n})\bar{K}_{b}(\bm{S}_{k}-\bm{S}_{\ell})\varepsilon_{n,i}\varepsilon_{n,j}\varepsilon_{n,k}\varepsilon_{n,\ell}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\neq j}\E\left[k_{J}^{2}(\bm{S}_{i}/A_{n})k_{J}^{2}(\bm{S}_{j}/A_{n})\bar{K}_{b}^{2}(\bm{S}_{i}-\bm{S}_{j})\varepsilon_{n,i}^{2}\varepsilon_{n,j}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=n(n-1)\int_{R_{0}^{2}}\bar{K}_{b}(A_{n}(\bm{z}_{1}-\bm{z}_{2}))k_{J}^{2}(\bm{z}_{1})k_{J}^{2}(\bm{z}_{2})\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}(\bm{z}_{1})\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}(\bm{z}_{2})A_{n}^{-2}g(\bm{z}_{1})g(\bm{z}_{2})d\bm{z}_{1}d\bm{z}_{2}

\displaystyle=n(n-1)A_{n}^{-1}b_{1}\dots b_{d}\int_{\bar{R}_{n}/\bm{b}}\bar{K}^{2}(\bm{v})\left(\int k_{J}^{2}\left(\bm{z}_{2}+{\bm{v}\circ\bm{b}\over A_{n}}\right)k_{J}^{2}(\bm{z}_{2})\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\times\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}\left(\bm{z}_{2}+{\bm{v}\circ\bm{b}\over A_{n}}\right)\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}\left(\bm{z}_{2}\right)g\left(\bm{z}_{2}+{\bm{v}\circ\bm{b}\over A_{n}}\right)g\left(\bm{z}_{2}\right)d\bm{z}_{2}\right)d\bm{v}\lesssim n(n-1)A_{n}^{-1}b_{1}\dots b_{d}J^{2}.

(C.28)

Then we have
